ParlaMint-DK_20201217220851	Thank you. New citizens believe that the Danes should be heard in a referendum on Denmark's accession to the EU recovery plan. For the first time, we Danes and our children must be liable for a loan made by the European Union. Joint debt creation will increase the pressure on the need for EU taxes. Denmark's commitment to the EU will increase significantly with the recovery plan. The European Commission has long been working for the EU to have its own tax revenues and an unnecessary precedent will be created. There is no guarantee that the Member States receiving grants or loans will carry out the necessary reforms to prevent countries from returning to an unfair economic situation. 750 billion. Euros, approx. 6,500 billion. kr., the Danes must repay our nice part of in the period from 2028 to 2058. With conservative mandates, it was ensured that the government could travel to Brussels with an absurd mandate... a mandate for the historic debt of the Danes. With the EU recovery plan, the Danes are committed not only to an increase in payments to the EU, the Prime Minister's accession to the Recovery Fund is also an flagrant breach of an earlier promise not to send any more money to the EU. I would like to thank the Left for not agreeing to give the government that mandate. In New Citizens we do not believe that the Danes should be enslaved to the EU. Two-and-a-half billion. kr. extra a year it will cost the Danish taxpayers for 30 years. In addition, in the agreement, the European Commission states in black and white that it will work for new revenue and a reform of the revenue base. It is a major democratic problem that the Danish Parliament and the government are giving away money, which the Danes have confidently paid in tax, naturally in the expectation that the tax crowns would go to our own welfare community, to hospitals, schools, care for the elderly, etc. for the benefit of us in Denmark. At the same time, the EU refuses to save on current expenditure, on the contrary. Britain has resigned, but the European Union completely ignores this. They merely increase the cost of the remaining countries. The European Union continues as if nothing had happened. Together with the proposal for a recovery fund that is on the table, Denmark is expected to increase expenditure on the EU by 23 per cent. Unfortunately, the Danish constitution does not oblige the Danish Parliament to hold a referendum on this increased debt obligations, but any reasonable and democratic idea advocates that the Danes be heard freely and directly in this matter, because the Danes have never been asked. Danish citizens have never been given the opportunity to take a position. Will we and our children really cut our own welfare society to support, for example. the southern European countries? Supporters may say that redistribution is necessary in the light of corona. That idea must be rejected. The money will only come out to work from the earliest in the summer of 2021. The money must be targeted at measures that emphasise everything but economic recovery, for example. climate action. It is doubtful that the aid package will work as intended. None of these extra corona money can change the self-inflicted suffering of the South European economies. Danes therefore do not pay for the recovery of the EU; Danes pay for early retirement, undeclared work and lack of moderation in southern Europe. The recovery that the European Union wants is a restoration of the image of the European Union, an image that has been damaged during the Corona crisis, because confidence in the European Union has once again suffered a break. The European Union is once again interfering without courage, the European Union is once again perplexing the necessary national measures. National self-determination, decentralisation and local democracy are de facto incompatible with the spirit and letter of the European Union. Money that goes from Danish welfare to central government in Brussels and to southern Europe cannot be further away from the citizens. The principle of subsidiarity is shattered by this centralist recovery plan. The European Union always wants more centralisation and power in Brussels. It is surprising that the government and, in particular, the Conservatives agree. If the Danes are to be responsible for a huge borrowing in the EU, the Folketing should now ask the Danes whether the Danes agree with this debt. That is why we are discussing this resolution today while time remains. All countries have a right of veto when it comes to the recovery plan. The recovery plan cannot enter into force unless all countries ratify it nationally. The Danes must be heard when they are strapped to the EU's debt union. They haven't been. That is the opportunity we will take, it is the opportunity to say no to giving away the Danes' money, we will make the subject of a referendum. Then I would like to thank the Danish People's Party and the Enhedslisten for supporting the resolution and thank you for the debate. Thank you for the floor.
